### Q84. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following statement is a tautology?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From De Morgan&#39;s Law,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>f</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mi>f</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>t</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi></math> is contradiction and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math> is tautology.</p>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi></math> and <math><mi>D</mi></math> be four non-empty sets. The contrapositive statement of &ldquo;If <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>D</mi></math> , then <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> &rdquo; is

**Options:**

- **(A)** If <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> , then <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>D</mi></math>
- **(B)** If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> , then <math><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8834;</mo><mi>A</mi></math> and <math><mi>D</mi><mo>&#8834;</mo><mi>B</mi></math>
- **(C)** If <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> , then <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>D</mi></math>
- **(D)** If <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> , then <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> or <math><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>D</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) If <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> , then <math><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> or <math><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8840;</mo><mi>D</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8838;</mo><mi>C</mi></math></p><p>Contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>R</mi></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>P</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>Q</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<table border="1px solid">	<thead>		<tr>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math></th>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math></th>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></th>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math></th>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math></th>			<th><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></th>		</tr>	</thead>	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>F</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>T</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>F</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>F</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>F</td>			<td>F</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>			<td>T</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

### Q88. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Boolean expression <math><mo>&#8764;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>(</mo><mo>∼</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>⇒</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>q</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mo>∼</mo><mi>p</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>

<details>

<p>Recall <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8764;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the truth value of the statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is false <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>F</mi></mfenced></math>, then the truth values of the statements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>r</mi></math> are respectively

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Recall, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is false if and only if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> is ### Q90. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The negation of the Boolean expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>r</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Negation of given statement is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using De Morgan&#39;s law <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>B</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using double negation law <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi></math>, and De Morgan&#39;s law</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi></math></p><p>Using distributive law <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced ### Q92. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math>, then the truth values of <math><mi>p</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>q</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>r</mi></math> are respectively, (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>e</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that the conditional statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>b</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math> only when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math></p><p>Again, we know that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>b</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi></math> when both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>b</mi></math> are false.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For any two statement <math><mi>p</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the negative of the expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8596;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo></math> we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using distributive law, i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> represents contradiction, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo></math> we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which one of the following statements is not a tautology?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>→</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>p</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Logical Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is false if and only if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> is true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> is false.</p><p>So, when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> is false, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> is true, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> will be false.</p><p>Clearly <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is not a tautology.</p>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The contrapositive of the statement &ldquo;If you are born in India, then you are a citizen of India&rdquo;, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** If you are not born in India, then you are not a citizen of India.
- **(B)** If you are a citizen of India, then you are born in India.
- **(C)** If you are born in India, then you are not a citizen of India.
- **(D)** If you are not a citizen of India, then you are not born in India.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) If you are not a citizen of India, then you are not born in India.**

**Detailed Solution:**

As we know, contrapositive of <math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is <math><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>p</mi></math>
Hence, required contrapositive statement is &ldquo;If you are not a citizen of India, then you are not born in India&rdquo;.

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is logically equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi> </mi><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi> </mi><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi> </mi><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>.</p><p>Hence, the expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is logically equivalent to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Boolean expression <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>∧</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4a9e6803-9c02-439c-b397-69a51d31235a-image.png" style="width: 270px; height: 207px;" /></p><p><math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>b</mi></math></p><p><math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>d</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

### Q98. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Contrapositive of the statement "If two numbers are not equal, then their squares are not equal". is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** If the squares of two numbers are not equal, then the numbers are equal.
- **(B)** If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are not equal.
- **(C)** If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are equal.
- **(D)** If the squares of two numbers are not equal, then the numbers are not equal.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are equal.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Contrapositive of "If $A$ then $B^{\prime \prime}$ is "If $\sim \mathrm{B}$ then $\sim A^{\prime \prime}$. Hence contrapositive of "If two numbers are not equal, then their squares are not equal" is "If squares of two numbers are equal, then the two numbers are equal".

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\mathrm{q}$ is false and $\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{q} \leftrightarrow \mathrm{r}$ is true, then which one of the following statements is a tautology?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(\mathrm{p} \vee \mathrm{r}) \rightarrow(\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{r})$
- **(B)** $\quad(\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{r}) \rightarrow(\mathrm{p} \vee \mathrm{r})$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{r}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{p} \vee \mathrm{r}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\quad(\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{r}) \rightarrow(\mathrm{p} \vee \mathrm{r})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$q$ is false and $[(p \wedge q) \leftrightarrow r]$ is true As $(p \wedge q)$ is false

$[$ False $\leftrightarrow r]$ is true

Hence $r$ is false

Option (1): says $p \vee r$,

Since $r$ is false

Hence $(p \vee r)$ can either be true or false

Option (2): says $(p \wedge r) \rightarrow(p \vee r)$

$(p \wedge r)$ is false

Since, $F \rightarrow T$ is true and

$F \rightarrow F$ is also true

Hence, it is a tautology

Option (3): $(p \vee r) \rightarrow(p \wedge r)$

i.e. $(p \vee r) \rightarrow F$

It can either be true or false

Option (4): $(p \wedge r)$

Since, $r$ is false

Hence, $(p \wedge r)$ is false.

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Consider the following three statements:</p><p><math><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math> is a prime number</p><p><math><mi>Q</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>7</mn></math> is a factor of <math><mn>192</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>LCM</mi></math> of <math><mn>5</mn></math> and <math><mn>7</mn></math> is <math><mn>35</mn></math></p><p>Then the truth value of which one of the following statements is true?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>P</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>P</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi>P</mi></math> is true</p><p><math><mi>Q</mi></math> is False</p><p><math><mi>R</mi></math> is True</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>F</mi></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>F</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the statement: &quot;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>n</mi></mfenced><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>41</mn></math> is prime&quot;. Then which one of the following is true?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mn>3</mn></mfenced></math> is false but <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is true
- **(B)** Both <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are false
- **(C)** Both <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are true
- **(D)** <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is false but <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>n</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>41</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>47</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mn>5</mn></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>61</mn></math></p><p>Hence <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> both are prime.</p><p>Hence, both are true.</p>

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The logical statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>r</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>By distributive law</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/399954-21.png" style="width: 400px; height: 126px;" /></p><p><mi><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math></mi></p><p><math><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></math></p><p>By Associative law</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p>

</details>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8743;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8743;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>

<p>1) <math ### Q104. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is false, then the truth values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> are, respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> has truth value false only when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> is true &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> is false. Hence, truth value of<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>p</mi></math> is true.

Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is false only when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math> &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>both are false.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> are both true.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q105. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is false, then the truth values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Recall <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>  if and only if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi></math><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p><p>Taking Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>A</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Hence, option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>A</mi></mfenced></math> is false.</p><p>Taking Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>B</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Hence, option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>B</mi></mfenced></math> is false.</p><p>Taking Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>C</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p><p>Hence, option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>C</mi></mfenced></math> is correct.</p><p>Taking Option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>D</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>F</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Hence, option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>D</mi></mfenced></math> is false.</p>

</details>

### Q106. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 1 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $(\mathrm{p} \wedge \sim \mathrm{q}) \wedge(\mathrm{p} \wedge \mathrm{r}) \rightarrow \sim p \vee q$ is false, then the truth values of $\mathrm{p}, \mathrm{q}$ and $\mathrm{r}$ are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{F}, \mathrm{T}, \mathrm{F}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{T}, \mathrm{F}, \mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** F, F, F
- **(D)** T, T, T

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{T}, \mathrm{F}, \mathrm{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the truth table for the $(p \wedge \sim q) \wedge(p \wedge r)$ $\rightarrow \sim p \vee q$ is false, then only possible values of $(p, q, r)$ is $(T, F, T)$
$\begin{array}{llllll}p & q & r & \sim q & p \wedge \sim q & p \wedge r \\ \sim p & \sim p \vee q & (p \wedge \sim q) \wedge(p \wedge r)(p \wedge \sim q) \wedge(p \wedge r) \\ \rightarrow \sim p \vee q\end{array}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/oaYv7WZEGTGzOfZsUc1Df8pk6nzdfvgi7W66eTy1CZs.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

- **(D)** <math><mi>q</mi></math>

### Q108. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The contrapositive of the statement &#39;If two numbers are not equal, then their squares are not equal&#39;, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are not equal
- **(B)** If the squares of two numbers are not equal, then the numbers are equal
- **(C)** If the squares of two numbers are not equal, then the numbers are not equal
- **(D)** If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are equal

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are equal**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math></p><p>Contrapositive is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math></p><p>So, answer is (d) - If the squares of two numbers are equal, then the numbers are equal.</p>

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The contrapositive of the following statement, &quot;If the side of a square doubles, then its area increases four times&quot;, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** if the area of a square increases four times, then its side is not doubled.
- **(B)** if the area of a square increases four times, then its side is doubled.
- **(C)** if the area of a square does not increase four times, then its side is not doubled.
- **(D)** if the side of a square is not doubled, then its area does not increase four times.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) if the area of a square does not increase four times, then its side is not doubled.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Contrapositive of <math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math>.

So, contrapositive of given statement is, &quot;If the area of a square does not increase four times, then its side is not doubled&quot;.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q112. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the following two statements:

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> : If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is an odd number, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is divisible by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> : If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is a prime number, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is an odd number.

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is the truth value of the contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> is the truth value of contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math>, then the ordered pair <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is not divisible by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn></math> is not odd number

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>F</mi><mfenced separators="|"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced></math>

Contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>:</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> is not odd number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn></math> is not a prime number  

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></math></p><p>Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q113. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Boolean Expression <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>p</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>p</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>∼</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>∼</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mi>p</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>p</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∧</mo><mi> </mi><mo>∼</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>∨</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>∼</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

Set equivalent 

<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∩</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mo>∪</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mi> </mi><mo>∩</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>∩</mo><mi>B)</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></math>

Hence answer is <math><mi>p</mi><mi> </mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>q</mi></math> .

</details>

---

### Q114. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the following statements:

P: Suman is brilliant

Q: Suman is rich

R: Suman is honest

The negation of the statement, &quot;Suman is brilliant and dishonest if and only if Suman is rich&quot; can be equivalently expressed as

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>↔</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>∨</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>∧</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>↔</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>∧</mo><mi>R</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mo>~</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>↔</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>∧</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>R</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> : Suman is brilliant 

<math><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>P</mi></math> : Suman is not brilliant

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> = Suman is rich 

<math><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>Q</mi></math> : Suman is not rich 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> : Suman is honest </p><p><math><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi></math> : Suman is not honest </p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8660;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8660;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8660;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>~</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#10231;</mo><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>&#10231;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#8764;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The contrapositive of the statement &quot;If it is raining, then I will not come&quot;, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** if I will come, then it is not raining.
- **(B)** if I will come, then it is raining.
- **(C)** if I will not come, then it is raining.
- **(D)** if I will not come, then it is not raining.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) if I will come, then it is not raining.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Contrapositive of <math><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>p</mi></math>.</p><p>So contrapositive of the statement &quot;If it is raining, then I will not come&quot;, would be, 
&quot;If I will come, then it is not raining&quot;.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The negation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8743;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8765;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>~</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8765;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8765;</mo><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8744;</mo><mi>F</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8743;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The contrapositive of the statement &#39;&#39;if I am not feeling well, then I will go to the doctor&quot; is

**Options:**

- **(A)** if I will go to the doctor, then I am not feeling well.
- **(B)** if I am feeling well, then I will not go to the doctor.
- **(C)** if I will not go to the doctor, then I am feeling well.
- **(D)** if I will go to the doctor, then I am feeling well.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) if I will not go to the doctor, then I am feeling well.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><strong>Statement:</strong> If I am not feeling well, then I will go to the doctor.</p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>:</mo></math> I am not feeling well and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi><mo>:</mo></math> I will go to the doctor.</p><p>The given statement is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math>.</p><p>We know that the contrapositive of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>q</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>~</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>~</mo><mi>p</mi></math>.</p><p><strong>Contrapositive:</strong> If I will not go to the doctor, then I am feeling well.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $\mathrm{p}, \mathrm{q}, \mathrm{r}$ denote arbitrary statements. Then the logically equivalent of the statement $\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow(\mathrm{q} \vee \mathrm{r})$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(\mathrm{p} \vee \mathrm{q}) \Rightarrow \mathrm{r}$
- **(B)** $(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{q}) \vee(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{r})$
- **(C)** $(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \sim \mathrm{q}) \wedge(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{r})$
- **(D)** $(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{q}) \wedge(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \sim \mathrm{r})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{q}) \vee(\mathrm{p} \Rightarrow \mathrm{r})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given statement is
$p \Rightarrow(q \vee r)$ which is equivalent to
$$
(p \Rightarrow q) \vee(p \Rightarrow r)
$$

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The proposition $\sim(p \vee \sim q) \vee \sim(p \vee q)$ is logically equivalent to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{p}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{q}$
- **(C)** $\sim \mathrm{p}$
- **(D)** $\sim \mathrm{q}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sim \mathrm{p}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\sim(p \vee \sim q) \vee \sim(p \vee q)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\equiv(\sim p \vee q) \vee(\sim p \vee \sim q) \\
&\equiv \sim p \vee(q \vee \sim q) \\
&\equiv \sim p
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $p$ and $q$ be any two logical statements and $\mathrm{r}: \mathrm{p} \rightarrow(\sim p \vee q)$. If $r$ has a truth value $F$, then the truth values of $p$ and $q$ are respectively:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{F}, \mathrm{F}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{T}, \mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** T, F
- **(D)** F, T

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) T, F**

**Detailed Solution:**

$p \rightarrow(\sim p \vee q)$ has truth value $\mathrm{F}$.
It means $p \rightarrow(\sim p \vee q)$ is false.
It means $p$ is true and $\sim p \vee q$ is false.
$\Rightarrow p$ is true and both $\sim p$ and $q$ are false.
$\Rightarrow p$ is true and $q$ is false.

</details>

---

### Q123. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For integers $m$ and $n$, both greater than 1, consider the following three statements :
$P: m$ divides $n$
$Q: m$ divides $n^2$
$R: m$ is prime, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $Q \wedge R \rightarrow P$
- **(B)** $P \wedge Q \rightarrow R$
- **(C)** $Q \rightarrow R$
- **(D)** $Q \rightarrow P$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $Q \wedge R \rightarrow P$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(b) $\frac{8}{4}=2, \frac{64}{4}=16$; but 4 is not prime.
Hence $\mathrm{P} \wedge \mathrm{Q} \rightarrow \mathrm{R}$, false
(c) $\frac{(6)^2}{12}=\frac{36}{12}=3$; but 12 is not prime Hence $Q \rightarrow R$, false
(d) $\frac{(4)^2}{8}=\frac{16}{8}=2 ; \frac{4}{8}$ is not an integer Hence $\mathrm{Q} \rightarrow \mathrm{P}$, false

</details>

### Q124. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The statement $p \rightarrow(q \rightarrow p)$ is equivalent to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $p \rightarrow q$
- **(B)** $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$
- **(C)** $p \rightarrow(p \rightarrow q)$
- **(D)** $p \rightarrow(p \wedge q)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dXOTGlO9WCEuETI6N7JbKjUCkFRRcxZoC_rIEjxGcfY.original.fullsize.png">

Since truth value of $p \rightarrow(q \rightarrow p)$ and $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$ are same, hence $p \rightarrow(q \rightarrow p)$ is equivalent to $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$.

</details>

### Q127. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $p$ and $q$ denote the following statements $p$ : The sun is shining
$q$ : I shall play tennis in the afternoon
The negation of the statement "If the sun is shining then I shall play tennis in the afternoon", is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $q \Rightarrow \sim p$
- **(B)** $q \wedge \sim p$
- **(C)** $p \wedge \sim q$
- **(D)** $\sim q \Rightarrow \sim p$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $p \wedge \sim q$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $p$ : The sun is shining. $q$ : I shall play tennis in the afternoon.
Negation of $p \rightarrow q$ is $\sim(p \rightarrow q)=p \wedge \sim q$

</details>

### Q128. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $p$ and $q$ be two Statements. Amongst the following, the Statement that is equivalent to $p \rightarrow q$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $p \wedge \sim q$
- **(B)** $\sim p \vee q$
- **(C)** $\sim p \wedge q$
- **(D)** $p \vee \sim q$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sim p \vee q$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $p$ and $q$ be two statements. $p \rightarrow q$ is equivalent to $\sim p \vee q$.

</details>

---

### Q129. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The logically equivalent preposition of $p \Leftrightarrow q$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(p \Rightarrow q \wedge) q \Rightarrow p \quad)$
- **(B)** $p \wedge q$
- **(C)** $(p \wedge q \vee) q \neq p \quad)$
- **(D)** $(p \wedge q \Rightarrow q \vee(p \quad)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(p \Rightarrow q \wedge) q \Rightarrow p \quad)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$(p \Rightarrow q) \wedge(q \Rightarrow p)$ means $p \Leftrightarrow q$

</details>

### Q130. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Statement that is TRUE among the following is

**Options:**

- **(A)** The contrapositive of $3 x+2=8 \Rightarrow x=2$ is $x \neq 2$ $\Rightarrow 3 x+2 \neq 8$.
- **(B)** The converse of $\tan \mathrm{x}=0 \Rightarrow x=0$ is $x \neq 0 \Rightarrow$ $\tan x=0$.
- **(C)** $p \Rightarrow q$ is equivalent to $p \vee \sim q$.
- **(D)** $p \vee q$ and $p \wedge q$ have the same truth table.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The contrapositive of $3 x+2=8 \Rightarrow x=2$ is $x \neq 2$ $\Rightarrow 3 x+2 \neq 8$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Only statement given in option
(a) is true.
(b) The converse of $\tan \mathrm{x}=0 \Rightarrow x=0$ is $x=0 \Rightarrow \tan x=0$
$\therefore$ Statement (b) is false
(c) $\sim(p \Rightarrow q$ is dquivalent to $p \wedge \sim q$
$\therefore$ Statement given in option (c) is false.
(d) No, $p \vee q$ and $p \wedge q$ does not have the same truth value.

</details>

### Q131. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The negation of the statement "If I become a teacher, then I will open a school" is

**Options:**

- **(A)** I will become a teacher and I will not open a school
- **(B)** Either I will not become a teacher or I will not open a school
- **(C)** Neither I will become a teacher nor I will open a school
- **(D)** I will not become a teacher or I will open a school

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) I will become a teacher and I will not open a school**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\sim(\sim p \vee q)=p \wedge \sim q$

</details>

### Q132. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the following statements
$\mathrm{P}$ : Suman is brilliant
Q : Suman is rich
$\mathrm{R}$ : Suman is honest
The negation of the statement "Suman is brilliant and dishonest if and only if Suman is rich" can be expressed as

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sim(\mathrm{Q} \leftrightarrow(\mathrm{P} \wedge \sim \mathrm{R}))$
- **(B)** $\sim Q \leftrightarrow \sim P \wedge R$
- **(C)** $\sim(P \wedge \sim R) \leftrightarrow Q$
- **(D)** $\sim P \wedge(Q \leftrightarrow \sim R)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sim(\mathrm{Q} \leftrightarrow(\mathrm{P} \wedge \sim \mathrm{R}))$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\sim\left\{\left(P_{\wedge} \sim R\right) \leftrightarrow Q\right\}=\sim\left\{Q \leftrightarrow\left(P_{\wedge} \sim R\right)\right\}$

</details>

### Q133. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Statement-1: $\sim(p \leftrightarrow \sim q)$ is equivalent to $p \leftrightarrow q$.
Statement-2 : $\sim(p \leftrightarrow \sim q)$ is a tautology.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is a correct explanation for Statement-1
- **(B)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is not a correct explanation for Statement-1
- **(C)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is false
- **(D)** Statement-1 is false, Statement-2 is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

### Q134. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The statement $p \rightarrow(q \rightarrow p)$ is equivalent to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $p \rightarrow(p \rightarrow q)$
- **(B)** $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$
- **(C)** $p \rightarrow(p \wedge q)$
- **(D)** $p \rightarrow(p \leftrightarrow q)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $p \rightarrow(p \vee q)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& p \rightarrow(q \rightarrow p)=\sim p \vee(q \rightarrow p) \\
& =\sim p \vee(\sim q \vee p) \quad \text { since } p \vee \sim p \text { is always true } \\
& =\sim p \vee p \vee q=p \rightarrow(p \vee q) .
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q135. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $p$ be the statement " $x$ is an irrational number", $q$ be the statement " $y$ is a transcendental number", and $r$ be the statement " $\mathrm{x}$ is a rational number iff $\mathrm{y}$ is a transcendental number".
Statement $-1$ : $r$ is equivalent to either $q$ or $p$
Statement $-2: r$ is equivalent to $\sim(p \leftrightarrow \sim q)$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement $-1$ is false, Statement $-2$ is true
- **(B)** Statement $-1$ is true, Statement $-2$ is true, Statement $-2$ is a correct explanation for Statement $-1$
- **(C)** Statement $-1$ is true, Statement $-2$ is true; Statement $-2$ is not a correct explanation for Statement $-1$.
- **(D)** Statement $-1$ is true, Statement $-2$ is false.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement $-1$ is true, Statement $-2$ is false.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given statement $r=\sim p \leftrightarrow q$
Statement $-1: r_1=(p \wedge \sim q) \vee(\sim p \wedge q)$
Statement $-2: r_2=\sim(p \leftrightarrow \sim q)=(p \wedge q) \vee(\sim q \wedge \sim p)$
From the truth table of $r, r_1$ and $r_2$, $r=r_1$.
Hence Statement $-1$ is true and Statement $-2$ is false.

</details>
